\doxysubsubsection*{:\+ The name that is attached to a reserved memory region}
reserve\+\_\+mem\+\_\+release\+\_\+by\+\_\+name -\/ Release reserved memory region with a given name

Forcibly release the pages in the reserved memory region so that those memory can be used as free memory. After released the reserved region size becomes 0.

Returns:\+ 1 if released or 0 if not found. \begin{DoxyCompactItemize}

\+\_\+\+\_\+memblock\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+or\+\_\+panic -\/ Try to allocate memory and panic on failure @size:\+ size of memory block to be allocated in bytes @align:\+ alignment of the region and block\textquotesingle{}s size @func:\+ caller func name

This function attempts to allocate memory using memblock\+\_\+alloc, and in case of failure, it calls panic with the formatted message. This function should not be used directly, please use the macro memblock\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+or\+\_\+panic.

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{memblock_8c_ace26aeff06aa20920192923daf46a9f1} 
void \+\_\+\+\_\+next\+\_\+mem\+\_\+range (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{u64 \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{idx}{, }\item[{int}]{nid}{, }\item[{enum memblock\+\_\+flags}]{flags}{, }\item[{struct memblock\+\_\+type \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{type\+\_\+a}{, }\item[{struct memblock\+\_\+type \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{type\+\_\+b}{, }\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{out\+\_\+start}{, }\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{out\+\_\+end}{, }\item[{int \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{out\+\_\+nid}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\+\_\+\+\_\+next\+\_\+mem\+\_\+range -\/ next function for for\+\_\+each\+\_\+free\+\_\+mem\+\_\+range() etc. @idx:\+ pointer to u64 loop variable @nid:\+ node selector, NUMA\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+NODE for all nodes @flags:\+ pick from blocks based on memory attributes @type\+\_\+a:\+ pointer to memblock\+\_\+type from where the range is taken @type\+\_\+b:\+ pointer to memblock\+\_\+type which excludes memory from being taken @out\+\_\+start:\+ ptr to phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t for start address of the range, can be NULL @out\+\_\+end:\+ ptr to phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t for end address of the range, can be NULL @out\+\_\+nid:\+ ptr to int for nid of the range, can be NULL

Find the first area from \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}@idx which matches @nid, fill the out parameters, and update \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}@idx for the next iteration. The lower 32bit of \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}@idx contains index into type\+\_\+a and the upper 32bit indexes the areas before each region in type\+\_\+b. For example, if type\+\_\+b regions look like the following,

0:\+\+[0-\/16), 1:\+\+[32-\/48), 2:\+\+[128-\/130)

The upper 32bit indexes the following regions.

0:\+\+[0-\/0), 1:\+\+[16-\/32), 2:\+\+[48-\/128), 3:\+\+[130-\/MAX)

As both region arrays are sorted, the function advances the two indices in lockstep and returns each intersection.

memblock\+\_\+add\+\_\+range -\/ add new memblock region @type:\+ memblock type to add new region into @base:\+ base address of the new region @size:\+ size of the new region @nid:\+ nid of the new region @flags:\+ flags of the new region

Add new memblock region \+[@base, @base + @size) into @type. The new region is allowed to overlap with existing ones -\/ overlaps don\textquotesingle{}t affect already existing regions. @type is guaranteed to be minimal (all neighbouring compatible regions are merged) after the addition.

Return:\+ 0 on success, -\/errno on failure.

memblock\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+exact\+\_\+nid\+\_\+raw -\/ allocate boot memory block on the exact node without zeroing memory @size:\+ size of memory block to be allocated in bytes @align:\+ alignment of the region and block\textquotesingle{}s size @min\+\_\+addr:\+ the lower bound of the memory region from where the allocation is preferred (phys address) @max\+\_\+addr:\+ the upper bound of the memory region from where the allocation is preferred (phys address), or MEMBLOCK\+\_\+\+ALLOC\+\_\+\+ACCESSIBLE to allocate only from memory limited by memblock.\+current\+\_\+limit value @nid:\+ nid of the free area to find, NUMA\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+NODE for any node

{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{memblock_8c_ac4b40f7ec95bc14ecd3d1a4708514f3d} 
void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\+\_\+\+\_\+init memblock\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+internal (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{size}{, }\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{align}{, }\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{min\+\_\+addr}{, }\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{max\+\_\+addr}{, }\item[{int}]{nid}{, }\item[{bool}]{exact\+\_\+nid}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

memblock\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+internal -\/ allocate boot memory block @size:\+ size of memory block to be allocated in bytes @align:\+ alignment of the region and block\textquotesingle{}s size @min\+\_\+addr:\+ the lower bound of the memory region to allocate (phys address) @max\+\_\+addr:\+ the upper bound of the memory region to allocate (phys address) @nid:\+ nid of the free area to find, NUMA\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+NODE for any node @exact\+\_\+nid:\+ control the allocation fall back to other nodes

Allocates memory block using \doxylink{memblock_8c_a23875c89e572709cf1ad3fe305b8dc52}{memblock\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+range\+\_\+nid()} and converts the returned physical address to virtual.

The @min\+\_\+addr limit is dropped if it can not be satisfied and the allocation will fall back to memory below @min\+\_\+addr. Other constraints, such as node and mirrored memory will be handled again in \doxylink{memblock_8c_a23875c89e572709cf1ad3fe305b8dc52}{memblock\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+range\+\_\+nid()}.

Return:\+ Virtual address of allocated memory block on success, NULL on failure.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{memblock\_alloc\_range\_nid()}{memblock\_alloc\_range\_nid()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{memblock_8c_a23875c89e572709cf1ad3fe305b8dc52} 
phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t \+\_\+\+\_\+init memblock\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+range\+\_\+nid (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{size}{, }\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{align}{, }\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{start}{, }\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{end}{, }\item[{int}]{nid}{, }\item[{bool}]{exact\+\_\+nid}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

memblock\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+range\+\_\+nid -\/ allocate boot memory block @size:\+ size of memory block to be allocated in bytes @align:\+ alignment of the region and block\textquotesingle{}s size @start:\+ the lower bound of the memory region to allocate (phys address) @end:\+ the upper bound of the memory region to allocate (phys address) @nid:\+ nid of the free area to find, NUMA\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+NODE for any node @exact\+\_\+nid:\+ control the allocation fall back to other nodes

The allocation is performed from memory region limited by memblock.\+current\+\_\+limit if @end == MEMBLOCK\+\_\+\+ALLOC\+\_\+\+ACCESSIBLE.

If the specified node can not hold the requested memory and @exact\+\_\+nid is false, the allocation falls back to any node in the system.

For systems with memory mirroring, the allocation is attempted first from the regions with mirroring enabled and then retried from any memory region.

In addition, function using kmemleak\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+phys for allocated boot memory block, it is never reported as leaks.

Return:\+ Physical address of allocated memory block on success, \%0 on failure.

memblock\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+try\+\_\+nid\+\_\+raw -\/ allocate boot memory block without zeroing memory and without panicking @size:\+ size of memory block to be allocated in bytes @align:\+ alignment of the region and block\textquotesingle{}s size @min\+\_\+addr:\+ the lower bound of the memory region from where the allocation is preferred (phys address) @max\+\_\+addr:\+ the upper bound of the memory region from where the allocation is preferred (phys address), or MEMBLOCK\+\_\+\+ALLOC\+\_\+\+ACCESSIBLE to allocate only from memory limited by memblock.\+current\+\_\+limit value @nid:\+ nid of the free area to find, NUMA\+\_\+\+NO\+\_\+\+NODE for any node

Public function, provides additional debug information (including caller info), if enabled. Does not zero allocated memory, does not panic if request cannot be satisfied.

Return:\+ Virtual address of allocated memory block on success, NULL on failure.

memblock\+\_\+double\+\_\+array -\/ double the size of the memblock regions array @type:\+ memblock type of the regions array being doubled @new\+\_\+area\+\_\+start:\+ starting address of memory range to avoid overlap with @new\+\_\+area\+\_\+size:\+ size of memory range to avoid overlap with

Double the size of the @type regions array. If memblock is being used to allocate memory for a new reserved regions array and there is a previously allocated memory range \+[@new\+\_\+area\+\_\+start, @new\+\_\+area\+\_\+start + @new\+\_\+area\+\_\+size]\+ waiting to be reserved, ensure the memory used by the new array does not overlap.

Return:\+ 0 on success, -\/1 on failure.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{memblock\_estimated\_nr\_free\_pages()}{memblock\_estimated\_nr\_free\_pages()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{memblock_8c_ad1acdc3f5ed7930724fc4df625700268} 
unsigned long \+\_\+\+\_\+init memblock\+\_\+estimated\+\_\+nr\+\_\+free\+\_\+pages (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

memblock\+\_\+estimated\+\_\+nr\+\_\+free\+\_\+pages -\/ return estimated number of free pages from memblock point of view

During bootup, subsystems might need a rough estimate of the number of free pages in the whole system, before precise numbers are available from the buddy. Especially with CONFIG\+\_\+\+DEFERRED\+\_\+\+STRUCT\+\_\+\+PAGE\+\_\+\+INIT, the numbers obtained from the buddy might be very imprecise during bootup.

Return:\+ An estimated number of free pages from memblock point of view.

int \+\_\+\+\_\+init\+\_\+memblock memblock\+\_\+isolate\+\_\+range (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct memblock\+\_\+type \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{type}{, }\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{base}{, }\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{size}{, }\item[{int \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{start\+\_\+rgn}{, }\item[{int \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{end\+\_\+rgn}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [static]}}

memblock\+\_\+isolate\+\_\+range -\/ isolate given range into disjoint memblocks @type:\+ memblock type to isolate range for @base:\+ base of range to isolate @size:\+ size of range to isolate @start\+\_\+rgn:\+ out parameter for the start of isolated region @end\+\_\+rgn:\+ out parameter for the end of isolated region

Walk @type and ensure that regions don\textquotesingle{}t cross the boundaries defined by \+[@base, @base + @size). Crossing regions are split at the boundaries, which may create at most two more regions. The index of the first region inside the range is returned in \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}@start\+\_\+rgn and the index of the first region after the range is returned in \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}@end\+\_\+rgn.

Return:\+ 0 on success, -\/errno on failure.

int \+\_\+\+\_\+init\+\_\+memblock memblock\+\_\+mark\+\_\+nomap (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{base}{, }\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{size}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

memblock\+\_\+mark\+\_\+nomap -\/ Mark a memory region with flag MEMBLOCK\+\_\+\+NOMAP. @base:\+ the base phys addr of the region @size:\+ the size of the region

The memory regions marked with MEMBLOCK\+\_\+\+NOMAP will not be added to the direct mapping of the physical memory. These regions will still be covered by the memory map. The struct page representing NOMAP memory frames in the memory map will be Page\+Reserved()

Note:\+ if the memory being marked MEMBLOCK\+\_\+\+NOMAP was allocated from memblock, the caller must inform kmemleak to ignore that memory

Return:\+ 0 on success, -\/errno on failure.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{memblock\_reserved\_mark\_noinit()}{memblock\_reserved\_mark\_noinit()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{memblock_8c_a11275d58e6dbd4228437d3f9f26180eb} 
int \+\_\+\+\_\+init\+\_\+memblock memblock\+\_\+reserved\+\_\+mark\+\_\+noinit (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{base}{, }\item[{phys\+\_\+addr\+\_\+t}]{size}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

memblock\+\_\+reserved\+\_\+mark\+\_\+noinit -\/ Mark a reserved memory region with flag MEMBLOCK\+\_\+\+RSRV\+\_\+\+NOINIT

@base:\+ the base phys addr of the region @size:\+ the size of the region

The struct pages for the reserved regions marked MEMBLOCK\+\_\+\+RSRV\+\_\+\+NOINIT will not be fully initialized to allow the caller optimize their initialization.

When CONFIG\+\_\+\+DEFERRED\+\_\+\+STRUCT\+\_\+\+PAGE\+\_\+\+INIT is enabled, setting this flag completely bypasses the initialization of struct pages for such region.

When CONFIG\+\_\+\+DEFERRED\+\_\+\+STRUCT\+\_\+\+PAGE\+\_\+\+INIT is disabled, struct pages in this region will be initialized with default values but won\textquotesingle{}t be marked as reserved.

Return:\+ 0 on success, -\/errno on failure.

\index{max\_low\_pfn@{max\_low\_pfn}!memblock.c@{memblock.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{max\_low\_pfn}{max\_low\_pfn}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{memblock_8c_ae12472a5d712c8ded1d522488983049a} 
unsigned long max\+\_\+low\+\_\+pfn}

DOC:\+ memblock overview

Memblock is a method of managing memory regions during the early boot period when the usual kernel memory allocators are not up and running.

Memblock views the system memory as collections of contiguous regions. There are several types of these collections:\+


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item {\ttfamily memory} -\/ describes the physical memory available to the kernel; this may differ from the actual physical memory installed in the system, for instance when the memory is restricted with {\ttfamily mem=} command line parameter
\item {\ttfamily reserved} -\/ describes the regions that were allocated
\item {\ttfamily \doxylink{memblock_8c_a678ba239c7672914f75167ea6b2fe8c7}{physmem}} -\/ describes the actual physical memory available during boot regardless of the possible restrictions and memory hot(un)plug; the {\ttfamily \doxylink{memblock_8c_a678ba239c7672914f75167ea6b2fe8c7}{physmem}} type is only available on some architectures.
\end{DoxyItemize}

Each region is represented by struct memblock\+\_\+region that defines the region extents, its attributes and NUMA node id on NUMA systems. Every memory type is described by the struct memblock\+\_\+type which contains an array of memory regions along with the allocator metadata. The "{}memory"{} and "{}reserved"{} types are nicely wrapped with struct memblock. This structure is statically initialized at build time. The region arrays are initially sized to INIT\+\_\+\+MEMBLOCK\+\_\+\+MEMORY\+\_\+\+REGIONS for "{}memory"{} and INIT\+\_\+\+MEMBLOCK\+\_\+\+RESERVED\+\_\+\+REGIONS for "{}reserved"{}. The region array for "{}physmem"{} is initially sized to INIT\+\_\+\+PHYSMEM\+\_\+\+REGIONS. The \doxylink{memblock_8c_a2f7c4876018ab925b1509288006e2a50}{memblock\+\_\+allow\+\_\+resize()} enables automatic resizing of the region arrays during addition of new regions. This feature should be used with care so that memory allocated for the region array will not overlap with areas that should be reserved, for example initrd.

The early architecture setup should tell memblock what the physical memory layout is by using \doxylink{memblock_8c_aa1c37d02973fd2f0eaf4f976ebe90ac5}{memblock\+\_\+add()} or \doxylink{memblock_8c_ab9238f8848df2efe3032fe285eb7eef6}{memblock\+\_\+add\+\_\+node()} functions. The first function does not assign the region to a NUMA node and it is appropriate for UMA systems. Yet, it is possible to use it on NUMA systems as well and assign the region to a NUMA node later in the setup process using \doxylink{memblock_8c_ad0e685a82a3772b25b78e30e34fa8595}{memblock\+\_\+set\+\_\+node()}. The \doxylink{memblock_8c_ab9238f8848df2efe3032fe285eb7eef6}{memblock\+\_\+add\+\_\+node()} performs such an assignment directly.

Once memblock is setup the memory can be allocated using one of the API variants:\+


\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item memblock\+\_\+phys\+\_\+alloc\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}() -\/ these functions return the {\bfseries{physical}} address of the allocated memory
\item memblock\+\_\+alloc\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}() -\/ these functions return the {\bfseries{virtual}} address of the allocated memory.
\end{DoxyItemize}

Note, that both API variants use implicit assumptions about allowed memory ranges and the fallback methods. Consult the documentation of \doxylink{memblock_8c_ac4b40f7ec95bc14ecd3d1a4708514f3d}{memblock\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+internal()} and \doxylink{memblock_8c_a23875c89e572709cf1ad3fe305b8dc52}{memblock\+\_\+alloc\+\_\+range\+\_\+nid()} functions for more elaborate description.

As the system boot progresses, the architecture specific mem\+\_\+init() function frees all the memory to the buddy page allocator.

Unless an architecture enables CONFIG\+\_\+\+ARCH\+\_\+\+KEEP\+\_\+\+MEMBLOCK, the memblock data structures (except "{}physmem"{}) will be discarded after the system initialization completes.
